\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

We have three maps
\overleftarrow{h}_{2},h_{2},\overrightarrow{h}_{2}:\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}\to\Bun_{G}\times C
defined by
\overrightarrow{h}_{2}:=\overrightarrow{h}\circ\overrightarrow{p}
,
\overleftarrow{h}_{2}:=\overleftarrow{h}\circ\overleftarrow{p}
, and
h_{2}:=\overleftarrow{h}\circ\overrightarrow{p}=\overrightarrow{h}\circ\overleftarrow{p}
. We also have a map
m:\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}\to\Hk_{G}
given by composition of modifications. We have a map
\Delta_{\widetilde{\Hk}}=(\mathrm{id},l\circ\overrightarrow{p}):\widetilde{\Hk}\to\widetilde{\Hk}\times C
.

Similarly, we have the local iterated Hecke stack defined by the Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}}

and maps
\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},2},h_{\mathrm{l},2},\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},2}:\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}\to\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))
,
m_{\mathrm{l}}:\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}\to\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}
. We have a map
f_{\Hk,2}:\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}\to\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}
. We have
\mathcal{K}_{1}*\mathcal{K}_{2}=m_{\mathrm{l},!}(\overleftarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{1}\otimes\overrightarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{2})
.

We would like to prove

\overleftarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(m_{1}\otimes m_{2})-\overrightarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(m_{1}\otimes m_{2})=\Delta_{\Hk,!}f^{*}_{\Hk}([\alpha]m_{1}\otimes m_{2})\in H^{*}(\Hk_{G}\times C,\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(\mathcal{K}_{1}*\mathcal{K}_{2})).

Since we have an isomorphism
r_{m}:H^{*}(\Hk_{G}\times C,\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(\mathcal{K}_{1}*\mathcal{K}_{2}))\cong H^{*}(\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}\times C,\widetilde{\pr}_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk,2}^{*}(\overleftarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{1}\otimes\overrightarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{2}))
where
\widetilde{\pr}_{1}:\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}\times C\to\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}
is the projection map to the first factor. The identity above is equivalent to

r_{m}(\overleftarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(m_{1}\otimes m_{2})-\overrightarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(m_{1}\otimes m_{2}))=r_{m}\Delta_{\Hk,!}f^{*}_{\Hk}([\alpha]m_{1}\otimes m_{2}).

Note that

\begin{split}&r_{m}(\overleftarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(m_{1}\otimes m_{2})-\overrightarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}(m_{1}\otimes m_{2}))\\
=&(\overleftarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\overleftarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{1}-\overrightarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{1})\cup(\overrightarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{2}\\
&+(\overleftarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{1}\cup(\overrightarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\overleftarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{2}-\overrightarrow{f}^{*}\alpha\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{2})\\
=&(\overleftarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\Delta_{\Hk,!}f^{*}_{\Hk}([\alpha]m_{1})\cup(\overrightarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{2}+(\overleftarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\pr_{1}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{1}\cup(\overrightarrow{p}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\Delta_{\Hk,!}f^{*}_{\Hk}([\alpha]m_{2})\\
=&\Delta_{\widetilde{\Hk},!}(\overleftarrow{p}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}([\alpha]m_{1})\cup\overrightarrow{p}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{2}+\overleftarrow{p}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}m_{1}\cup\overrightarrow{p}^{*}f_{\Hk}^{*}([\alpha]m_{2}))\\
=&\Delta_{\widetilde{\Hk},!}f_{\Hk,2}^{*}(\overleftarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}([\alpha]m_{1})\cup\overrightarrow{p}^{*}_{\mathrm{l}}m_{2}+\overleftarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}m_{1}\cup\overrightarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}([\alpha]m_{2}))\\
=&\Delta_{\widetilde{\Hk},!}f_{\Hk,2}^{*}r_{m_{\mathrm{l}}}([\alpha]m_{1}\otimes m_{2})\\
=&r_{m}\Delta_{\Hk,!}f_{\Hk}^{*}([\alpha]m_{1}\otimes m_{2}).\end{split}

Here, we are using the isomorphism
r_{m_{\mathrm{l}}}:H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}},\mathcal{K}_{1}*\mathcal{K}_{2})\cong H^{*}(\widetilde{\Hk}_{G}^{\mathrm{l}},\overleftarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{1}\otimes\overrightarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{2})
and the identity
m_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}[\alpha]=\overleftarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}[\alpha]+\overrightarrow{p}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}[\alpha]
in the next to the last step. This finishes the proof.

3.5.5.
Conclude the proof

First, note that the images of
\phi_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}:H^{*}(\mathbb{B}(L^{+}\GL_{N}\times\Aut(D)))\to H^{*}(\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D)))
for all homomorphisms
G\to\GL_{N}
generate
H^{*}(\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D)))
as an algebra. By the functoriality and multiplicativity, we are reduced to proving Theorem
3.2
for
\GL_{N}
. By the equivalent formulation using perverse sheaves and propagation under convolution, we are reduced to showing Theorem
3.8
when
\mathcal{K}=\IC_{(1,0,\cdots,0)}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{\GL_{N}})^{\heartsuit}
. This case follows from
[
3
, Theorem 3.2.1]
, which can also be verified via a straightforward computation.

4.
Langlands dual description of eigenweights

In this section, we explain the local volume datum associated with the determinant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\det}\in\Pic(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
and a coweight
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
as promised in Example
2.2
. Moreover, when
G
is simple, we give a description of the constants
b_{\lambda},\epsilon_{\lambda,i}
involved in Theorem
1.2
on the Langlands dual side.

4.1.
Local volume datum for the determinant line bundle

We now consider a specific choice of local volume datum defined in Definition
2.1
.

4.1.1.
The determinant line bundle

We first recall the construction of the determinant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\det}\in\Pic(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
. It is characterized as follows. Let

q:L^{+}G\backslash LG\to\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}

be the natural map. We require that
q^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}\in\Pic(L^{+}G\backslash LG)\subset\Pic(L^{+}G\backslash LG)_{\mathbb{Q}}
is ample and generates
\Pic(L^{+}G\backslash LG^{e})
for each
e\in\pi_{0}(LG)
. Here,
LG^{e}
is the connected component of
LG
indexed by
e
. Moreover,
\mathcal{L}_{\det}
is relatively ample with respect to the map

\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}:\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}\to\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))

in (
2.4
).

We now recall an explicit construction of
\mathcal{L}_{\det}
. For each finite-dimensional representation
V\in\Rep(G)
with representation map
r_{V}:G\to\GL(V)
, we obtain an induced map

\phi_{V}:\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}\to\Hk_{\GL(V)}^{\mathrm{l}}.

We identify
\Hk_{\GL(V)}^{\mathrm{l}}
with the moduli stack of modifications
\{\mathcal{E}_{1}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{2}\}
of vector bundles of rank
\dim(V)
over
D
considered modulo
\Aut(D)
, and we adopt the convention that the map

\overrightarrow{h}_{\GL(V),\mathrm{l}}:\Hk_{\GL(V)}^{\mathrm{l}}\to\mathbb{B}(L^{+}\GL(V)\rtimes\Aut(D))

sends
(\mathcal{E}_{1}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{2})
to
\mathcal{E}_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\Std}
be the line bundle on
\Hk_{\GL(V)}^{\mathrm{l}}
whose fiber at
(\mathcal{E}_{1}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{2})
is

(\mathcal{E}_{2}:\mathcal{E}_{1}):=\det(\mathcal{E}_{2}/\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\otimes\det(\mathcal{E}_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})^{-1}.

Define
\mathcal{L}_{V}:=\phi_{V}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Std}\in\Pic(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})
.

Let
\Ad\in\Rep(G)
denote the adjoint representation. The above construction yields a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\Ad}\in\Pic(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})
. We then define the determinant line bundle by

\mathcal{L}_{\det}:=\mathcal{L}_{\Ad}^{\otimes 1/(2h_{G}^{\vee})}\in\Pic(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})_{\mathbb{Q}},

where
h_{G}^{\vee}
is the dual Coxeter number of
G
.

4.1.2.
Local volume data

For each dominant coweight
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
, define

(4.1)

d_{\lambda}:=\langle 2\rho,\lambda\rangle.

We use
\IC_{\lambda}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})
to denote the intersection complex of the closed Schubert cell
\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}\subset\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}
normalized such that
\IC_{\lambda}\langle-d_{\lambda}\rangle|_{\Hk_{G,\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}}
lies in the heart of the naive
t
-structure. Under the isomorphism

(4.2)

\begin{split}\Hom^{0}(\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\IC_{\lambda}\langle d_{\lambda}\rangle,\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))})\cong\Hom^{0}(\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\underline{k}_{\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}}\langle 2d_{\lambda}\rangle,\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))})\cong k\end{split},

we use
[\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}]^{BM}\in\Hom^{0}(\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\IC_{\lambda}\langle d_{\lambda}\rangle,\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))})
to denote the element corresponding to
1\in k
, which is the
(relative) fundamental (Borel-Moore homology) class
of
\Hk_{G,\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}
(over
\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}
).

Consider the first Chern class
c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})\in H^{2}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})
. We define

\mathfrak{c}_{\lambda,\det}^{\mathrm{l}}:=c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})^{d_{\lambda}+1}\cdot[\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}]^{BM}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}},\IC_{\lambda}\langle-d_{\lambda}-2\rangle}(\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))},\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))}).

In other words, the element

\mathfrak{c}_{\lambda,\det}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\Hom^{0}(\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\IC_{\lambda}\langle-d_{\lambda}-2\rangle,\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))})

is given by the composition

\begin{split}\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\IC_{\lambda}\langle-d_{\lambda}-2\rangle\xrightarrow{\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}(-\cup c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})^{d_{\lambda}+1})}\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\IC_{\lambda}\langle d_{\lambda}\rangle\xrightarrow{[\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}]^{BM}}\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))}\end{split}.

Consider also the (relative) fundamental (cohomology) class (over
\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}
)

\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}:=[\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}]\in\Hom^{0}(\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))},\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\IC_{\lambda}\langle-d_{\lambda}\rangle),

that is, the element corresponds to
1\in k
under the isomorphism

\Hom^{0}(\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))},\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}\IC_{\lambda}\langle-d_{\lambda}\rangle)\cong\Hom^{0}(\underline{k}_{\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))},\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},*}\underline{k}_{\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}})\cong k.

Definition 4.1
.

The
local volume datum given by the determinant line bundle
is the triple

v_{\lambda,\det}=(\IC_{\lambda},\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\lambda,\det},\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\lambda})

in which each term is defined as above.

4.2.
Translation to the spectral side

In this section, we describe the constants
b_{\lambda}
and
\epsilon_{\lambda,i}
appearing in Theorem
1.2
in terms of the Langlands dual group. For simplicity, we assume that
G
is almost simple.

4.2.1.
Notations

Let
\kappa_{\min}:\check{\mathfrak{g}}\times\check{\mathfrak{g}}\to k
be the
\check{G}
-invariant non-degenerate bilinear form normalized such that
\kappa_{\min}(\alpha_{\mathrm{long}},\alpha_{\mathrm{long}})=2
where
\alpha_{\mathrm{long}}\in X_{*}(\check{T})
is any long coroot of
\check{G}
.

For each finite-dimensional representation
V\in\Rep(\check{G})
, let
r_{V}:\check{G}\to\GL(V)
be the representation map and
dr_{V}:\check{\mathfrak{g}}\to\mathfrak{gl}(V)
its differential. This defines a
\check{G}
-invariant bilinear form

(4.3)

\kappa_{V}:\check{\mathfrak{g}}\times\check{\mathfrak{g}}\to k,\qquad\kappa_{V}(X,Y)=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(dr_{V}(X)\,dr_{V}(Y)\bigr)

for
X,Y\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}
. Let
\Ad\in\Rep(\check{G})
denote the adjoint representation. Then

(4.4)

\kappa_{\min}=\frac{\kappa_{\Ad}}{2h^{\vee}_{\check{G}}l_{G}}.

Here
l_{G}
is the lacing number, equal to
1
for types
ADE
,
2
for types
BCF
, and
3
for type
G
, and
h_{\check{G}}^{\vee}
is the dual Coxeter number of
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
.

We use
\check{\alpha}_{j}\in X^{*}(\check{T}),\,1\leq j\leq n
to denote the simple roots of
\check{G}
. By restriction, we can regard
\kappa_{\min}
as a
W
-invariant bilinear form on
\check{\mathfrak{t}}
(hence also
\mathfrak{t}\cong\check{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
) satisfying
\kappa_{\min}(\check{\alpha}_{\mathrm{short}},\check{\alpha}_{\mathrm{short}})=2
where
\check{\alpha}_{\mathrm{short}}\in X^{*}(\check{T})
is any short root of
\check{G}
. That is, the bilinear form
\kappa_{\min}
corresponds to the basic bilinear form on
\mathfrak{g}
.

We use
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
to denote the Chevalley form of
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
. Choose Chevalley generators
x_{i}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{Z},\alpha_{i}},\penalty\ 1\leq i\leq n
which are unique up to signs. Let
e=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\kappa_{\min}(\check{\alpha}_{i},\check{\alpha}_{i})x_{i}/2\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
\rho\in X_{*}(\check{T})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset\check{\mathfrak{t}}
be the half sum of all positive coroots of
\check{G}
. This determines an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple
(e,2\rho,f)
for a unique
f\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Using the non-degenerate bilinear form
\kappa_{\min}
, we make identification
\check{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
. Consider the Kostant slice
\check{\mathfrak{s}}=e+\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}\subset\check{\mathfrak{g}}
. We have a canonical grading
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}=\bigoplus_{d}\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,d}
where
[\rho,-]
restricts to
\cdot d
on
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,d}
.
9
9

9

The
d
’s appearing here are the negative of
exponents
of
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
.
Then we have
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}
when
G
is not of type
D_{n}
and
n
is even.

Let
\check{\mathfrak{c}}=\check{\mathfrak{g}}\sslash\check{G}
be the Chevalley quotient and
\chi:\check{\mathfrak{g}}\to\check{\mathfrak{c}}
be the quotient map. It induces an isomorphism
\chi|_{\check{\mathfrak{s}}}:\check{\mathfrak{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\check{\mathfrak{c}}
. Moreover, we have
\check{\mathfrak{s}}\cong\check{\mathfrak{c}}\cong\mathbb{V}
as graded vector spaces after properly normalizing the grading.

Let
J_{\check{G}}\to\check{\mathfrak{s}}
be the regular centralizer whose fiber at
X\in\check{\mathfrak{s}}
is the centralizer subgroup
C_{\check{G}}(X)\subset\check{G}
. The map
\chi
induces a map
d\chi:\check{\mathfrak{g}}\times_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}}T^{*}\check{\mathfrak{c}}\to T^{*}\check{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\check{\mathfrak{g}}\times\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\cong\check{\mathfrak{g}}\times\check{\mathfrak{g}}
which restricts to an isomorphism
d\chi|_{\check{\mathfrak{s}}}:T^{*}\check{\mathfrak{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Lie J_{\check{G}}
.

The bilinear form
\kappa_{\min}
restricts to a non-degenerate bilinear form
\kappa:=\kappa_{\min}|_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}\times\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e}}:\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}\times\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e}\to k
. Using this, we make identification
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e}\cong\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}^{*}
. Note that we also have a grading
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e}=\bigoplus_{d}\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,d}
such that we have
(\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,-d})^{*}\cong\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,d}
.

We use
V_{\lambda}\in\Rep(\check{G})
to denote the irreducible representation of
\check{G}
with highest weight
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
. We use
V_{\lambda,\mathbb{Z}}\subset V_{\lambda}
to denote the standard representation of
\check{G}_{\mathbb{Z}}
with highest weight
\lambda
, which is a
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice inside
V_{\lambda}
. We use
u_{\lambda,-}\in V_{\lambda,\mathbb{Z}}
to denote a generator of the lowest weight submodule, and
u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\in V_{\lambda,\mathbb{Z}}^{*}
to denote a generator of the lowest weight submodule of
V_{\lambda,\mathbb{Z}}^{*}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(V_{\lambda,\mathbb{Z}},\mathbb{Z})
. The generators
u_{\lambda,-}
and
u_{\lambda,+}^{*}
are normalized such that
\langle e^{d_{\lambda}}\cdot u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle\geq 0
. This determines the pair
u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}
up to a simultaneous sign change. Let
V_{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{\mu\in X^{*}(\check{T})}V_{\lambda}(\mu)
be the weight space decomposition.

4.2.2.
Spectral description of
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}

Now we describe the eigenweights
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}
involved in Theorem
1.2
(see also Example
2.8
) as some invariants attached to the representation
V_{\lambda}\in\Rep(\check{G})
.

Consider the bilinear form
\kappa_{\lambda}:\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}\times\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e}\to k
defined by

(4.5)

\kappa_{\lambda}(X,Y)=\langle\sum_{s=0}^{d_{\lambda}}e^{s}XYe^{d_{\lambda}-s}\cdot u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle.

This determines an endomorphism
E_{\lambda}\in\End(\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f})=\End(\check{\mathfrak{s}})
defined such that
\kappa(E_{\lambda}(Y),X)=\kappa_{\lambda}(Y,X)
for any
X\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e}
and
Y\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}
.

Theorem 4.2
.

Under the natural identification
\mathbb{V}\cong\check{\mathfrak{s}}
, we have
E_{v_{\lambda,\det}}=-E_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

During this proof, we exchange the role of
\overleftarrow{h}
and
\overrightarrow{h}
everywhere to match the convention in most literature, like
[
2
]
[
9
]
. In particular, we change the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\det}
such that it is relatively ample over
\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}
. Since
E_{\lambda}=E_{-w_{0}(\lambda)}
, we can still work with
\lambda
instead of
-w_{0}(\lambda)
.

Recall that we use
\kappa_{\min}
to make identification
\check{\mathfrak{c}}^{*}\cong\check{\mathfrak{c}}
.
By
[
2
, Theorem 1(b)]
, there is a canonical graded isomorphism
H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})\cong\bigoplus_{z\in Z(\check{G})}\mathcal{O}(D_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}}(\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}}))
.
10
10

10

By
\pi_{0}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})\cong\pi_{1}(G)\cong X^{*}(Z(\check{G}))
, we get a connected component decomposition
\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}}=\coprod_{\chi\in X^{*}(Z(\check{G}))}\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l},\chi}
. Note that different
H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l},\chi})
for
\chi\in X^{*}(Z(\check{G}))
are canonically isomorphic. This gives a canonical diagonal embedding
H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l},\mathrm{triv}})\subset H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})
for
\mathrm{triv}\in X^{*}(Z(\check{G}))
. Under the normalization we choose, this embedding corresponds to
\mathcal{O}(D_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}}(\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}}))\subset\bigoplus_{z\in Z(\check{G})}\mathcal{O}(D_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}}(\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}}))
which is the inclusion via the direct summand indexed by the identity element
e\in Z(\check{G})
.

Here, we use
D_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}}(\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}})
to denote the deformation to the normal cone of the scheme
\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}}
along the closed subscheme
\check{\mathfrak{c}}\subset\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}}
embedding along the diagonal. Under this identification, the natural maps
\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*},\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}:H^{*}(\mathbb{B}(L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D)))\to H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})
get identified with pull-back maps for the natural projections
p_{1},p_{2}:D_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}}(\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}})\to\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
where we identify
\mathbb{A}^{1}\cong\Spec k[\hbar]
.

The canonical isomorphism
H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})\otimes_{k[\hbar]}k\cong H^{*}(L^{+}G\backslash LG/L^{+}G)
gets identified with the isomorphism
\bigoplus_{z\in Z(\check{G})}\mathcal{O}(D_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}}(\check{\mathfrak{c}}\times\check{\mathfrak{c}})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\{0\})\cong\bigoplus_{z\in Z(\check{G})}\mathcal{O}(T\check{\mathfrak{c}})\cong\bigoplus_{z\in Z(\check{G})}U(\Lie J_{\check{G}})
. Under this identification, for
x\in\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{c}})
, the image of
[x]=(\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}x-\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}x)/\hbar
in
H^{*}(L^{+}G\backslash LG/L^{+}G)
is the differential
1
-form
-dx\in\Gamma(\check{\mathfrak{c}},\Omega_{\check{\mathfrak{c}}})\subset\mathcal{O}(T\check{\mathfrak{c}})\subset\bigoplus_{z\in Z(\check{G})}\mathcal{O}(T\check{\mathfrak{c}})
where the last inclusion is via the direct summand indexed by the identity element
e\in Z(\check{G})
.
11
11

11

To see this subtle minus sign here, consider the filtration
F_{\geq\mu}I\!H^{*}(T\backslash\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\subset I\!H^{*}(T\backslash\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
as in the proof of Theorem
4.4
. One can check that taking cup product with the image of
[x]
in
H^{*}(T\backslash\Gr_{G}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
coincides with the multiplication by
-\partial_{\lambda}x\in\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{t}})
on the graded piece
\Gr_{\mu}^{F}I\!H^{*}(T\backslash\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
where
\partial_{\lambda}x
is the partial derivative of
x\in\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{c}})\subset\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{t}})
along
\lambda\in\mathfrak{t}\cong\check{\mathfrak{t}}
.

The first Chern class
c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})\in H^{*}(L^{+}G\backslash LG/L^{+}G)
is identified with the tautological section
e^{\mathrm{univ}}:=(X\in\check{\mathfrak{s}}\mapsto X\in\Lie J_{\check{G}})\in\Lie J_{\check{G}}\subset U(\Lie J_{\check{G}})
.

By
[
2
, Theorem 4]
, the graded
H^{*}(L^{+}G\backslash LG/L^{+}G)
-module
I\!H^{*}(L^{+}G\backslash\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda})
gets identified with the graded vector space
V_{\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{s}})
equipped with the tautological graded
\bigoplus_{z\in Z(\check{G})}U(\Lie J_{\check{G}})
-action. Under this identification, we can choose the lowest weight generators properly such that
[\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}]\penalty\ \text{mod $\hbar$}=u_{\lambda,-}\otimes 1\in V_{\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{s}})
and
[\Hk_{G,\leq\lambda}^{\mathrm{l}}]^{BM}\penalty\ \text{mod $\hbar$}=u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\otimes 1\in V_{\lambda}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{s}})
.

Therefore, the map
E_{v_{\lambda,\det}}:\check{\mathfrak{s}}\to\check{\mathfrak{s}}
is determined as follows: For
x\in\check{\mathfrak{s}}\cong\check{\mathfrak{s}}^{*}
, we have

(4.6)

\langle(e^{\mathrm{univ}})^{d_{\lambda}+1}(-dx)\cdot(u_{\lambda,-}\otimes 1),u^{*}_{\lambda,+}\otimes 1\rangle=E_{v_{\lambda,\det}}(x)+\textup{other terms not in $\check{\mathfrak{s}}^{*}\subset\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{s}})$}.

To simplify notations, we assume
G
is not of type
D_{2n}
. (This case can be treated similarly) Under this assumption, we have
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}
and each graded piece
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}
is one-dimensional. Therefore, we can choose non-zero elements
f_{j}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}
, and we have
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}=\Span(f_{j})
. We have
e^{\mathrm{univ}}=e+\sum_{j}a_{j}f_{j}
where
a_{j}\in(\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1})^{*}\subset\check{\mathfrak{s}}^{*}
are homogeneous generators of
\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{s}})
and
f_{j}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}
. Since
e^{d_{\lambda}+1}
acts by zero on
V_{\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{O}(\check{\mathfrak{s}})
, from (
4.6
), we get

(4.7)

E_{v_{\lambda},\det}(a_{j})=-\langle\sum_{s=0}^{d_{\lambda}+1}e^{s}f_{j}e^{d_{\lambda}+1-s}(da_{j})|_{e\in\check{\mathfrak{s}}}\cdot u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle\cdot a_{j}

where
(da_{j})|_{e\in\check{\mathfrak{s}}}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,d_{j}-1}
. Therefore, we only need to show that
\kappa_{\min}((da_{j})|_{e\in\check{\mathfrak{s}}},f_{j})=1
. This translates to the fact that
a_{j}
regarded as a function on
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
has derivative at
e\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}
along direction
f_{j}
equals
1
, which follows from the tautological relation
a_{j}(e+\alpha f_{j})=\alpha
for
\alpha\in k
.
∎

Theorem
4.2
has the following immediate corollary:

Corollary 4.3
.

When
G
is not of type
D_{n}
for even
n
, we have
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}=\frac{\kappa_{\lambda}|_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}\otimes\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,d_{j}-1}}}{\kappa_{\min}|_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}\otimes\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,d_{j}-1}}}\in\mathbb{Q}
for
1\leq j\leq n
.

4.2.3.
Spectral description of
b_{\lambda}

In this subsection, we describe the number
b_{\lambda}\in\mathbb{Q}
involved in Theorem
1.2
(see also Example
2.8
) as an invariant attached to the representation
V_{\lambda}\in\Rep(\check{G})
.

Theorem 4.4
.

We have

b_{\lambda}=\langle\sum_{s=0}^{d_{\lambda}}e^{s}H_{2}e^{d_{\lambda}-s}u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle\in\mathbb{Q}

where
H_{2}=(\sum_{\alpha\in\Phi_{G}}(d\alpha)^{2})/{4h^{\vee}_{G}}\in U(\check{\mathfrak{t}})\subset U(\check{\mathfrak{g}})
,
h^{\vee}_{G}
is the dual Coxeter number of
G
.

Proof.

As we did in the proof of Theorem
4.2
, we exchange the role of
\overleftarrow{h}
and
\overrightarrow{h}
everywhere.

Note that we can work with
H^{*}(\Gr_{G}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
instead of
H^{*}(\Hk_{G}^{\mathrm{l}})
where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\Gr_{G}
via loop rotation. By the proof of
[
9
, Lemma 2.2]
, there is a canonical isomorphism
I\!H^{*}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong V_{\lambda}\otimes k[\hbar]
. Moreover, let
T_{\mu}=LN^{-}t^{\mu}L^{+}G/L^{+}G\subset\Gr_{G}
and
\overline{T_{\mu}}
be its closure for
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
. Define

F^{\geq\mu}I\!H^{*}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})=\mathrm{im}(I\!H^{*}_{(\overline{T_{\mu}}\cap\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda})/\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\to I\!H^{*}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})).

This gives a filtration
\{F^{\geq\mu}I\!H^{*}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\subset I\!H^{*}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\}_{\mu\in X_{*}(T)}
. On the spectral side, this filtration corresponds to the obvious filtration by weights
\{(G^{\geq\mu}V_{\lambda})\otimes k[\hbar]\}_{\mu\in X^{*}(\check{T})}
. Passing to associated graded pieces, this isomorphism gives
\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu}F(I\!H^{*}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m}))\cong i_{\mu}^{*}j_{\mu}^{!}\IC_{\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\otimes H^{*}(\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong V_{\lambda}(\mu)\otimes k[\hbar]
. Here
i_{\mu}:\{t_{\mu}\}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T_{\mu}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
j_{\mu}:T_{\mu}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
are the natural inclusions.

Note that taking cup product with
c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})
preserves the filtration
F^{\geq\mu}I\!H^{*}((L^{+}G\backslash LG_{\leq\lambda})/\mathbb{G}_{m})
. Therefore, we get an induced endomorphism
\cup c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})
on the associated graded pieces
\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu}F(I\!H^{*}(\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m}))
, which coincides with
\cup c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{[\{t^{\mu}\}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]})
on
i_{\mu}^{*}j_{\mu}^{!}\IC_{\Gr_{G,\leq\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\otimes H^{*}(\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{m})
. Note that
[\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{[\{t^{\mu}\}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}]\in\Pic(\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{m})_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathbb{Q}
can be identified with
-(\sum_{\alpha\in\Phi_{G}}\langle\alpha,\mu\rangle^{2})/4h^{\vee}_{G}=-\kappa_{\min}(\mu,\mu)/2
.

On the other hand, we know that
\cup c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})
modulo
\hbar
can be identified with the action of
e\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}
on
V_{\lambda}
. Unwinding the construction, we know
b_{\lambda}
is the coefficient of
\hbar
in
-\langle c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\det})^{d_{\lambda}+1}\cdot u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle\in k[\hbar]
. The desired identity follows immediately.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

Here is another description of
E_{\lambda}
and
b_{\lambda}
, which reveals their relation to relative Langlands duality as developed in
[
1
]
. We follow the notation of
[
7
, §4]
. Let
\check{M}=T^{*}_{\psi}\check{N}\cong\check{G}\times\check{\mathfrak{s}}
be the
\check{G}
-Hamiltonian space corresponding to the Whittaker period. It is the relative Langlands dual of the trivial
G
-Hamiltonian space
M=T^{*}X
for
X=*
. The elements
\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\lambda,\det}
and
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\lambda}
give rise to maps

\alpha:V_{\lambda}\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
and
\beta:V_{\lambda}^{*}\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
.
Reducing modulo
\hbar
and using the isomorphism
\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\otimes_{k[\hbar]}k\cong\mathcal{O}(\check{M})
, we obtain maps

\overline{\alpha}:V_{\lambda}\to\mathcal{O}(\check{M})
and
\overline{\beta}:V_{\lambda}^{*}\to\mathcal{O}(\check{M})
. Consider the canonical element
\mathrm{unit}\in V_{\lambda}\otimes V_{\lambda}^{*}
. Then

m\circ(\alpha\otimes\beta)(\mathrm{unit})=b_{\lambda}\cdot\hbar\in\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
,
where
m:\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\otimes\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
is the multiplication map. Define a vector field

X_{\lambda}:=\overline{m}\circ(d\otimes\mathrm{id})\circ(\overline{\alpha}\otimes\overline{\beta})(\mathrm{unit})\in\Gamma(\check{M},T^{*}\check{M})\cong\Gamma(\check{M},T\check{M})
,
where
d:\mathcal{O}(\check{M})\to\Gamma(\check{M},T^{*}\check{M})
is the de Rham differential and
\overline{m}:\mathcal{O}(\check{M})\otimes\Gamma(\check{M},T^{*}\check{M})\to\Gamma(\check{M},T^{*}\check{M})
is the multiplication map.
The vector field
X_{\lambda}
vanishes at
(\mathrm{id},e)\in\check{G}\times\check{\mathfrak{s}}\cong\check{M}
, and hence induces a Hessian endomorphism

H_{X_{\lambda},(\mathrm{id},e)}:T_{(\mathrm{id},e)}\check{M}\to T_{(\mathrm{id},e)}\check{M}
.
Under the identification
T_{(\mathrm{id},e)}\check{M}\cong\check{\mathfrak{g}}\times\check{\mathfrak{s}}
, one has

H_{X_{\lambda},(\mathrm{id},e)}=0\times E_{\lambda}\in\End(\check{\mathfrak{g}}\times\check{\mathfrak{s}})
.
This description can be further simplified by replacing
\check{M}
with its Whittaker reduction

T^{*}((\check{N},\psi)\backslash\check{G}/(\check{N},\psi))\cong J_{\check{G}}
.
It turns out that such Hessians controlling arithmetic intersection numbers of special cycles constitute a general phenomenon for hyperspecial varieties. We plan to investigate this direction in future work.

5.
Examples

In this section, we work out the constants
\epsilon_{\lambda,i},b_{\lambda}
appearing in Theorem
1.2
explicitly in some cases.

5.1.
Examples of
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}

In this section, we give examples of the number
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}\in\mathbb{Q}
for
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
and
1\leq j\leq n
, where
n
is the rank of
G
.

5.1.1.
Reduction to fundamental weights

The computation of the numbers
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}
can be reduced to the computation of invariants of fundamental weights
\varpi_{i}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
. In fact, the computation for
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
can be reduced to
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
. This is provided by Proposition
5.1
.

Consider
S_{\lambda}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e}^{*}
defined such that for
Y\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,d_{j}-1}
, one has

(5.1)

S_{\lambda}(Y)=\langle e^{d_{\lambda}-d_{j}+1}Y\cdot u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle.

Similarly, one define
T_{\lambda}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f}^{*}
such that for
X\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}
, one has

(5.2)

T_{\lambda}(X)=\langle\sum_{s=0}^{d_{\lambda}+d_{j}-1}e^{s}Xe^{d_{\lambda}+d_{j}-1-s}\cdot u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle.

Recall that we introduced the notation
d_{\lambda}=\langle 2\rho,\lambda\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Define
\deg_{\lambda}:=\langle e^{d_{\lambda}}u_{\lambda,-},u_{\lambda,+}^{*}\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We write
\kappa_{\lambda,j}:=\kappa_{\lambda}|_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}\otimes\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,d_{j}-1}}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

For
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
, we have

(5.3)

\begin{split}\kappa_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2},j}=\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{1}}+1}\kappa_{\lambda_{1},j}\deg_{\lambda_{2}}+\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{2}}+1}\kappa_{\lambda_{2},j}\deg_{\lambda_{1}}\\
+\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{1}}-d_{j}+1}T_{\lambda_{2},j}\otimes S_{\lambda_{1},j}+\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{2}}-d_{j}+1}T_{\lambda_{1},j}\otimes S_{\lambda_{2},j}\end{split}

(5.4)

S_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2},j}=\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}-d_{j}+1}{d_{\lambda_{1}}-d_{j}+1}S_{\lambda_{1},j}\deg_{\lambda_{2}}+\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}-d_{j}+1}{d_{\lambda_{2}}-d_{j}+1}S_{\lambda_{2},j}\deg_{\lambda_{1}}

(5.5)

T_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2},j}=\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{j}}{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{j}}T_{\lambda_{1},j}\deg_{\lambda_{2}}+\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{j}}{d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{j}}T_{\lambda_{2},j}\deg_{\lambda_{1}}

(5.6)

\deg_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}=\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}}{d_{\lambda_{1}}}\deg_{\lambda_{1}}\deg_{\lambda_{2}}.

This proposition reduces the computation of
\kappa_{\lambda}
for an arbitrary
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})
to the computation of
\kappa_{\varpi_{i}},S_{\varpi_{i}},T_{\varpi_{i}},\deg_{\varpi_{i}}
where
\varpi_{i}\in X^{*}(\check{T})
are the fundamental weights of
\check{G}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

5.1.2.
Case
j=1

In this subsection, we derive a formula of
\epsilon_{\lambda,1}
for any
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
. We treat this independently since the answer in this case is particularly simple.

Proposition 5.2
.

For any
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
, we have

(5.7)

\epsilon_{\lambda,1}=\frac{2h^{\vee}_{\check{G}}l_{G}d_{\lambda}(d_{\lambda}+1)(d_{\lambda}+2)\deg_{\lambda}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}(2d_{j}-2)(2d_{j}-1)2d_{j}}.

Proof.

Note that
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-1}=\Span(f)
and
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,1}=\Span(e)
. We get

\epsilon_{\lambda,1}=\kappa_{\lambda}(f,e)/\kappa_{\min}(f,e).

Consider the Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}=\Span(e,f,2\rho)\subset\check{\mathfrak{g}}
. We have

\Ad|_{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\Sym^{2d_{i}-2}\Std_{2}.

Note that

\kappa_{\lambda}(f,e)=\kappa_{\SL_{2},\Sym^{d_{\lambda}}\Std_{2}}(f,e)\cdot\deg_{\lambda}

where
\kappa_{\SL_{2},\Sym^{d_{\lambda}}\Std_{2}}
is the invariant bilinear form for
\SL_{2}
defined in (
4.3
) and

\kappa_{\min}(f,e)=\frac{1}{2h^{\vee}_{\check{G}}l_{G}}\kappa_{\Ad}(f,e)=\frac{1}{2h^{\vee}_{\check{G}}l_{G}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\kappa_{\SL_{2},\Sym^{2d_{j}-2}\Std_{2}}(f,e).

For any pair of integers
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, an easy computation shows

\frac{\kappa_{\SL_{2},\Sym^{b}\Std_{2}}}{\kappa_{\SL_{2},\Sym^{a}\Std_{2}}}=\frac{b(b+1)(b+2)}{a(a+1)(a+2)}.

This implies that

\epsilon_{\lambda,1}=\frac{\kappa_{\lambda}(f,e)\deg_{\lambda}}{\kappa_{\min}(f,e)}=\frac{2h^{\vee}_{\check{G}}l_{G}d_{\lambda}(d_{\lambda}+1)(d_{\lambda}+2)\deg_{\lambda}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}(2d_{i}-2)(2d_{i}-1)2d_{i}}.

∎

5.1.3.
Cyclic case

In this subsection, we illustrate a special situation where all the invariants
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}
for different
1\leq j\leq n
are the same. Recall we have the representation map
r_{V_{\lambda}}:\check{G}\to\GL(V_{\lambda})
whose differential is a Lie algebra homomorphism
dr_{V_{\lambda}}:\check{\mathfrak{g}}\to\mathfrak{gl}(V_{\lambda})
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose the element
dr_{V_{\lambda}}(e)\in\mathfrak{gl}(V_{\lambda})
is a regular nilpotent element, then we have
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}=\epsilon_{\lambda,1}
for any
1\leq j\leq n
.

Proof.

Under the assumption, we have
V_{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{s=0}^{d_{\lambda}}k\cdot e^{s}v_{\lambda,-}
. This implies that for any
X\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{e,d_{j}-1}
and
Y\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{f,-d_{j}+1}
, we have

\kappa_{\lambda}(X,Y)=\operatorname{tr}((dr_{V_{\lambda}})(X)\cdot(dr_{V_{\lambda}})(Y))\deg_{\lambda}=\kappa_{V_{\lambda}}(X,Y)\deg_{\lambda}

where
\kappa_{V_{\lambda}}
is defined in (
4.3
). This shows that
\kappa_{\lambda}=\frac{\kappa_{V_{\lambda}}\deg_{\lambda}}{\kappa_{\min}}\cdot\kappa_{\min}
, which implies
\epsilon_{\lambda,j}=\frac{\kappa_{V_{\lambda}}\deg_{\lambda}}{\kappa_{\min}}
for any
1\leq j\leq n
.
∎

The assumption of Proposition
5.3
is satisfied only in the following cases:

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c}\check{G}&\textup{Name of $\lambda$}&\epsilon_{\lambda,1}\\
\hline\cr A_{n-1}&\textup{standard}&1\\
C_{n}&\textup{standard}&4\\
G_{2}&\textup{quasi-minuscule}&108\end{array}.

For classical groups, one can write down explicit formulas for all the invariants involved in Proposition
5.1
. Compared to the formulas in
[
4
]
, these formulas are more elementary.

5.1.4.
Type
A_{n}

In this subsection, we determine all the invariants in Proposition
5.1
when
G=\PGL_{n+1}
. In this case, we have
d_{j}=j+1
for
1\leq j\leq n
. We have
\check{G}=\SL_{n+1}
. We order the fundamental weights as

We have
V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}=\bigwedge^{k}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{n+1,\mathbb{Z}}\in\Rep(\SL_{n+1,\mathbb{Z}})
where
\Std_{n+1,\mathbb{Z}}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n+1}\mathbb{Z}\cdot u_{i}
is the standard representation. We choose the usual maximal torus of
\SL_{n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
with respect to this basis. The invariant bilinear form is given by
\kappa_{\min}=\kappa_{\Std_{n+1}}:\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1}\times\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1}\to k
.

Take
e\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
such that

e\cdot u_{i}=\begin{cases}u_{i+1},&1\leq i\leq n,\\[2.0pt]
0,&i=n+1.\end{cases}.

We can choose lowest weight (co)vectors of
V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}
for
1\leq k\leq n
as

u_{\varpi_{k},-}=u_{n-k+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n+1}\in V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}

and

u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k})^{*}\in(V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}})^{*},

where the later is given by extracting the coefficient of
u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k}
under the standard basis of
\bigwedge^{k}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
. With this choice, one easily checks that
\langle e^{d_{\varpi_{k}}}\cdot u_{\varpi_{k},-},u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}\rangle>0
.

For the computation of
S_{\varpi_{k},j},T_{\varpi_{k},j}
, we choose basis of one-dimensional vector spaces
\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1,e,j},\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1,f,-j}
via:

(5.8)

e_{j}:=e^{(j)}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1,e,j}

(5.9)

f_{j}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1,f,-j},\penalty\ \kappa_{\Std_{n+1}}(f_{j},e_{j})=1

where
e^{(j)}
means the
j
-th power of
e
regarded as a
(n+1)\times(n+1)
-matrix.

In this way, define
s_{\lambda,j}:=S_{\lambda}(e_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
and
t_{\lambda,j}:=T_{\lambda}(f_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
. We have

s_{\varpi_{k},j}=\langle e^{s-j}e_{j}\cdot(u_{n-k+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n+1}),(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k})^{*}\rangle

t_{\varpi_{k},j}=\langle\sum_{s=0}^{k(n+1-k)+j}e^{s}f_{j}e^{k(n+1-k)+j-s}\cdot(u_{n-k+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n+1}),(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k})^{*}\rangle

\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},j}=\langle\sum_{s=0}^{k(n+1-k)}e^{s}f_{j}e^{k(n+1-k)-s}e_{j}\cdot(u_{n-k+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n+1}),(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k})^{*}\rangle.

Proposition 5.4
.

For
1\leq k\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq n
, We have

(5.10)

d_{\varpi_{k}}=kk^{\prime}

(5.11)

\deg_{\varpi_{k}}=\frac{(kk^{\prime})!}{\prod_{i=1}^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}}(i+j-1)}

(5.12)

\begin{split}s_{\varpi_{k},j}=\sum_{a=1}^{k}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\binom{kk^{\prime}-j}{k^{\prime}+(\sigma(1)-1),\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(a)-a)-j,\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(k)-k)}\end{split}

(5.13)

\begin{split}t_{\varpi_{k},j}=\sum_{b=1}^{k}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\binom{kk^{\prime}+j+1}{k^{\prime}+(\sigma(1)-1),\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(b)-b)+j+1,\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(k)-k)}\end{split}

(5.14)

\begin{split}\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},j}=\sum_{a=1}^{k}\sum_{b=1}^{k}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\cdot\\
\binom{kk^{\prime}+1}{k^{\prime}+(\sigma(1)-1),\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(a)-a)-j,\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(b)-b)+j+1,\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(k)-k)}\end{split}

where
k^{\prime}=n+1-k
. Here, the multinomial coefficient is taken to be zero if any of the entries is negative. In the formula (
5.14
), when
a=b
, the multinomial coefficient is understood as

\binom{kk^{\prime}+1}{k^{\prime}+(\sigma(1)-1),\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(a)-a)+1,\cdots,k^{\prime}+(\sigma(k)-k)}.

The proof of these formulas is elementary, and we omit it.

Remark 5.5
.

The formula (
5.14
) should be compared with the
[
4
, Theorem 1.3.2]
.

5.1.5.
Type
B_{n}

In this subsection, we consider the case
G=\mathrm{SO}_{2n+1}
in which case
d_{j}=2j
for
1\leq j\leq n
. We have
\check{G}=\Sp_{2n}
. We order the fundamental weights as

Define
\widetilde{V}_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}=\bigwedge^{k}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}\in\Rep(\Sp_{2n,\mathbb{Z}})
for
1\leq k\leq n
. Here
\widetilde{V}_{\varpi_{1},\mathbb{Z}}=\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2n}\mathbb{Z}\cdot u_{i}
is the standard representation of
\Sp_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
, and we choose the maximal torus of
\Sp_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
given by the prescribed basis of
\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
. The module
\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
carries a symplectic form
\omega
satisfying

\omega(u_{i},u_{j})=\begin{cases}1,&i+j=2n+1,\,1\leq i\leq n,\\[2.0pt]
0,&i+j\neq 2n+1.\end{cases}

Then
\Sp_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}=\Aut(\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}},\omega)
. The invariant bilinear form is given by
\kappa_{\min}=\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{\Std_{2n}}
.

Take
e\in\mathfrak{sp}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
such that

e\cdot u_{i}=\begin{cases}u_{i+1},&1\leq i\leq n-1,\\[2.0pt]
2u_{i+1},&i=n,\\[2.0pt]
-u_{i+1},&n+1\leq i\leq 2n-1,\\[2.0pt]
0,&i=2n.\end{cases}

We choose lowest weight (co)vectors

u_{\varpi_{k},-}=(-1)^{k(2n-k-1)/2}u_{2n-k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{2n}\in\widetilde{V}_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}

and

u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k})^{*}\in(\widetilde{V}_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}

for
1\leq k\leq n
. Take
V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}\subset\widetilde{V}_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}
to be the sub-representation of
\Sp_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
generated by
u_{\varpi_{k},-}\in\widetilde{V}_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}
. With this choice, one easily checks that
\langle e^{d_{\varpi_{k}}}\cdot u_{\varpi_{k},-},u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}\rangle>0
.

We choose basis of one-dimensional vector spaces
\mathfrak{sp}_{2n,e,2j-1},\mathfrak{sp}_{2n,f,1-2j}
via:

(5.15)

e_{j}:=e^{(2j-1)}\in\mathfrak{sp}_{2n,e,2j-1}

(5.16)

f_{j}\in\mathfrak{sp}_{2n,f,1-2j},\,\kappa_{\Std_{2n}}(f_{j},e_{j})=2

as in type
A
case. Define
s_{\lambda,j}:=S_{\lambda}(e_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
and
t_{\lambda,j}=T_{\lambda}(f_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Proposition 5.6
.

For
1\leq k\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq n
, we have

(5.17)

d_{\varpi_{k}}=k(2n-k)

(5.18)

\deg_{\varpi_{k}}=2^{k}\deg^{A_{2n-1}}_{\varpi_{k}}

(5.19)

s_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k}s^{A_{2n-1}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1}

(5.20)

t_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k+1}t^{A_{2n-1}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1}

(5.21)

\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k+1}\epsilon^{A_{2n-1}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1}

where we add superscript
A_{2n-1}
to denote the corresponding invariant of type
A_{2n-1}
given in Proposition
5.4
.

This is an immediate consequence of the corresponding result in type
A
given in Proposition
5.4
.

5.1.6.
Type
C_{n}

In this subsection, we consider the case
G=\mathrm{PSp}_{2n}
in which case
d_{j}=2j
for
1\leq j\leq n
. We have
\check{G}=\mathrm{Spin}_{2n+1}
. We order the fundamental weights as

Define
V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}=\bigwedge^{k}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
for
1\leq k\leq n-1
and
V_{2\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}=\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
. Here
V_{\varpi_{1},\mathbb{Z}}=\Std_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2n+1}\mathbb{Z}\cdot u_{i}
is the standard representation of
\SO_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
. We choose the maximal torus of
\mathrm{Spin}_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
determined by the basis of
\Std_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
. The module
\Std_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
carries a quadratic form
q
with the corresponding symmetric bilinear form
(x,y)=q(x+y)-q(x)-q(y)
satisfying

(u_{i},u_{j})=\begin{cases}1,&i+j=2n+2,\,i\neq n+1,\\[2.0pt]
2,&i=j=n+1,\\[2.0pt]
0,&i+j\neq 2n+2.\end{cases}

Then
\SO_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}=\Aut(\Std_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}},q)^{\circ}
and
\mathrm{Spin}_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
is the universal covering group of
\SO_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
. The invariant bilinear form is
\kappa_{\min}=\frac{1}{4}\kappa_{\Std_{2n+1}}
.

Take
e\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}}
such that

e\cdot u_{i}=\begin{cases}2u_{i+1},&1\leq i\leq n-1,\\[2.0pt]
u_{i+1},&i=n,\\[2.0pt]
-2u_{i+1},&n+1\leq i\leq 2n,\\[2.0pt]
0,&i=2n+1.\end{cases}

We can choose lowest weight (co)vectors

u_{\varpi_{k},-}=(-1)^{k(2n-k+1)/2}u_{2n-k+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{2n+1}\in V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}

u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k})^{*}\in(V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}

for
1\leq k\leq n-1
, and

u_{2\varpi_{n},-}=(-1)^{n(n+1)/2}u_{n+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{2n+1}\in V_{2\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}

u_{2\varpi_{n},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n})^{*}\in(V_{2\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}.

One can take
V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}\in\Rep(\mathrm{Spin}_{2n+1,\mathbb{Z}})
with lowest weight (co)vectors

u_{\varpi_{n},-}\in V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}},\,u_{\varpi_{n},+}^{*}\in(V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}

equipped with an injection
V_{2\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}\subset V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}^{\otimes 2}
such that

u_{\varpi_{n},-}^{\otimes 2}=u_{2\varpi_{n},-},\,(u_{\varpi_{n},+}^{*})^{\otimes 2}=u_{2\varpi_{n},+}^{*}.

One easily checks that
\langle e^{d_{\varpi_{k}}}\cdot u_{\varpi_{k},-},u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}\rangle>0
for
1\leq k\leq n
.

We choose basis of one-dimensional vector spaces
\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1,e,2j-1},\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1,f,1-2j}
via:

(5.22)

e_{j}:=e^{(2j-1)}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1,e,2j-1}

(5.23)

f_{j}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1,f,1-2j},\,\kappa_{\Std_{2n+1}}(f_{j},e_{j})=4.

Define
s_{\lambda,j}:=S_{\lambda}(e_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
and
t_{\lambda,j}=T_{\lambda}(f_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Proposition 5.7
.

For
1\leq k\leq n-1
and
1\leq j\leq n
, we have

(5.24)

d_{\varpi_{k}}=k(2n+1-k)

(5.25)

\deg_{\varpi_{k}}=2^{k(2n-k)}\deg^{A_{2n}}_{\varpi_{k}}

(5.26)

s_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k(2n-k)}s^{A_{2n}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1}

(5.27)

t_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k(2n-k)+2}t^{A_{2n}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1}

(5.28)

\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k(2n-k)+2}\epsilon^{A_{2n}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1}.

For
k=n
and
1\leq j\leq n
, we have

(5.29)

d_{\varpi_{n}}=n(n+1)/2

(5.30)

\deg_{\varpi_{n}}=(\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n}}}{d_{\varpi_{n}}}^{-1}2^{n^{2}}\deg_{\varpi_{n}}^{A_{2n}})^{1/2}

(5.31)

s_{\varpi_{n},j}=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n}}-2j+1}{d_{\varpi_{n}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n}}^{-1}2^{n^{2}-1}s^{A_{2n}}_{\varpi_{n},2j-1}

(5.32)

t_{\varpi_{n},j}=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n}}+2j}{d_{\varpi_{n}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n}}^{-1}2^{n^{2}+1}t^{A_{2n}}_{\varpi_{n},2j-1}

(5.33)

\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},j}=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n}}^{-1}(2^{n^{2}+1}\epsilon^{A_{2n}}_{\varpi_{n},2j-1}-\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n}}-2j+1}s_{\varpi_{n},j}t_{\varpi_{n},j})

This follows immediately from Proposition
5.4
and Proposition
5.1
.

Remark 5.8
.

The formula (
5.33
) should be compared with the
[
4
, Theorem 1.3.6]
.

5.1.7.
Type
D_{n}

In this subsection, we consider the case
G=\mathrm{PSO}_{2n}
in which case we arrange
d_{j}=2j
for
1\leq j\leq n-1
and
d_{n}=n
. Note that when
n
is even, we have
d_{n/2}=d_{n}=n
. We have
\check{G}=\mathrm{Spin}_{2n}
whose fundamental weights are ordered as

{}{}\hbox to85.84pt{\vbox to31.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.80165pt\lower-13.50064pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to0.0pt{{

\lxSVG@closescope {{

Define
V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}=\bigwedge^{k}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}\in\Rep(\mathrm{Spin}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}})
for
1\leq k\leq n-2
and
V_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}=\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}\in\Rep(\mathrm{Spin}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}})
. Here
V_{\varpi_{1},\mathbb{Z}}=\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2n}k\cdot u_{i}
is the standard representation of
\SO_{2n}
. We choose the maximal torus of
\mathrm{Spin}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
determined by the basis of
\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
. The module
\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
carries a quadratic form
q
with the corresponding symmetric bilinear form
(x,y)=q(x+y)-q(x)-q(y)
satisfying

(u_{i},u_{j})=\begin{cases}1,&i+j=2n+1,\\[2.0pt]
0,&i+j\neq 2n+1.\end{cases}

Then
\SO_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}=\Aut(\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}},q)^{\circ}
and
\mathrm{Spin}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
is the universal covering group of
\SO_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
. The invariant bilinear form on
\mathfrak{so}_{2n}
in given by
\kappa_{\min}=\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{\Std_{2n}}
.

Take
e\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
such that

e\cdot u_{i}=\begin{cases}u_{i+1},&1\leq i\leq n-2,\\[2.0pt]
u_{n}+u_{n+1},&i=n-1,\\[2.0pt]
-u_{n+2},&i=n,\\[2.0pt]
-u_{i+1},&n+1\leq i\leq 2n-1,\\[2.0pt]
0,&i=2n.\end{cases}

We can choose lowest weight (co)vectors

u_{\varpi_{k},-}=(-1)^{k(2n-k-1)/2}u_{2n-k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{2n}\in V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}}

u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{k})^{*}\in(V_{\varpi_{k},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}

for
1\leq k\leq n-2
, and

u_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},-}=(-1)^{n(n-1)/2}u_{n+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{2n}\in V_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}

u_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n-1})^{*}\in(V_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}.

Consider elements

u_{2\varpi_{n-1},-}=(-1)^{n(n-1)/2}u_{n+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{2n}\in\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}

u_{2\varpi_{n-1},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n})^{*}\in(\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}})^{*}

u_{2\varpi_{n},-}=(-1)^{n(n-1)/2}u_{n}\wedge u_{n+2}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{2n}\in\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}

u_{2\varpi_{n},+}^{*}=(u_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge u_{n-1}\wedge u_{n})^{*}\in(\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}})^{*}.

Let
V_{2\varpi_{n-1},\mathbb{Z}},\,V_{2\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}\subset\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
be the sub-representation of
\mathrm{Spin}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
generated by
u_{2\varpi_{n-1},-},\,u_{2\varpi_{n},-}\in\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{Z}}\Std_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
, respectively. With these choices, one can take
V_{\varpi_{n-1},\mathbb{Z}},\,V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}\in\Rep(\mathrm{Spin}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}})
with lowest weight (co)vectors

u_{\varpi_{n-1},-}\in V_{\varpi_{n-1},\mathbb{Z}},\,u_{\varpi_{n-1},+}^{*}\in(V_{\varpi_{n-1},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}

u_{\varpi_{n},-}\in V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}},\,u_{\varpi_{n},+}^{*}\in(V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}})^{*}

such that there exists embeddings
V_{2\varpi_{n-1},\mathbb{Z}}\subset V_{\varpi_{n-1},\mathbb{Z}}^{\otimes 2},\,V_{2\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}\subset V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}^{\otimes 2}
such that

u_{\varpi_{n-1},-}^{\otimes 2}=u_{2\varpi_{n-1},-},\,(u_{\varpi_{n-1},+}^{*})^{\otimes 2}=u_{2\varpi_{n-1},+},\,u_{\varpi_{n},-}^{\otimes 2}=u_{2\varpi_{n},-},\,(u_{\varpi_{n},+}^{*})^{\otimes 2}=u_{2\varpi_{n},+}.

Moreover, there exists embedding
V_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}\subset V_{\varpi_{n-1},\mathbb{Z}}\otimes V_{\varpi_{n},\mathbb{Z}}
such that

u_{\varpi_{n-1},-}\otimes u_{\varpi_{n},-}=u_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},-},\,u_{\varpi_{n-1},+}^{*}\otimes u_{\varpi_{n},+}^{*}=u_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},+}^{*}.

With these choices, one easily checks that
\langle e^{d_{\varpi_{k}}}\cdot u_{\varpi_{k},-},u_{\varpi_{k},+}^{*}\rangle>0
for
1\leq k\leq n
.

For
1\leq j\leq n-1
, define
e_{j}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n,e,2j-1},f_{j}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n,f,1-2j}
via:

(5.34)

e_{j}:=e^{(2j-1)}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n,e,2j-1}

(5.35)

f_{j}\in\Span(f^{(2j-1)})\subset\mathfrak{so}_{2n,f,1-2j},\,\kappa_{\Std_{2n}}(f_{j},e_{j})=2.

For
j=n
, take
e_{n}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n,e,n-1}
such that

e_{n}\cdot u_{i}=\begin{cases}u_{n}-u_{n+1},&i=1,\\[2.0pt]
u_{2n},&i=n,\\[2.0pt]
-u_{2n},&i=n+1,\\[2.0pt]
0,&\textup{else}\\[2.0pt]
\end{cases}

and
f_{n}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n,f,1-n}
such that

f_{n}\cdot u_{i}=\begin{cases}u_{1}/2,&i=n,\\[2.0pt]
-u_{1}/2,&i=n+1,\\[2.0pt]
(u_{n}-u_{n+1})/2,&i=2n,\\[2.0pt]
0,&\textup{else}.\\[2.0pt]
\end{cases}

Then one has
\kappa_{\Std_{2n}}(f_{n},e_{n})=2
. Moreover, when
n
is even, one has
\kappa_{\Std_{2n}}(f_{n},e_{n/2})=\kappa_{\Std_{2n}}(f_{n/2},e_{n})=0
. Define
s_{\lambda,j}:=S_{\lambda}(e_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
and
t_{\lambda,j}=T_{\lambda}(f_{j})\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Take
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathrm{Spin}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}})
to be the unique non-trivial outer automorphism fixing the pinning determined by
e\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n,\mathbb{Z}}
and the first vertex of the Dynkin diagram. We have
\sigma(e_{j})=e_{j}
,
\sigma(f_{j})=f_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq n-1
,
\sigma(e_{n})=-e_{n}
, and
\sigma(f_{n})=-f_{n}
.

When
n
is odd, we have
\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},j}=\kappa_{\varpi_{k}}(e_{j},f_{j})
for all
j,k
. When
n
is even, the same formula holds for
j\neq n/2,n
or
k\neq n-1,n
while the numbers
\epsilon_{\lambda,n/2}
and
\epsilon_{\lambda,n}
for
\lambda=\varpi_{n-1},\varpi_{n}
are eigenvalues of the
2\times 2
-matrix

(5.36)

\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{\lambda}(f_{n/2},e_{n/2})&\kappa_{\lambda}(f_{n},e_{n/2})\\
\kappa_{\lambda}(f_{n/2},e_{n})&\kappa_{\lambda}(f_{n},e_{n})\end{pmatrix}.

Proposition 5.9
.

For
1\leq k\leq n-2
and
1\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

(5.37)

d_{\varpi_{k}}=k(2n-1-k),

(5.38)

\deg_{\varpi_{k}}=2^{k}\deg^{A_{2n-2}}_{\varpi_{k}},

(5.39)

s_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k}s^{A_{2n-2}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1},

(5.40)

t_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k+1}t^{A_{2n-2}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1},

(5.41)

\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},j}=2^{k+1}\epsilon^{A_{2n-2}}_{\varpi_{k},2j-1}.

For
1\leq k\leq n-2
and
j=n
, we have

(5.42)

s_{\varpi_{k},n}=0,

(5.43)

t_{\varpi_{k},n}=0,

(5.44)

\epsilon_{\varpi_{k},n}=2^{k}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\binom{k(2n-1-k)+1}{2n-k+(\sigma(1)-1),2n-k-1+(\sigma(2)-2),\cdots,2n-k-1+(\sigma(k)-k)}.

For
k=n-1,n
and
1\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

(5.45)

d_{\varpi_{k}}=n(n-1)/2,

(5.46)

\deg_{\varpi_{k}}=(\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}2^{n-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{A_{2n-2}})^{1/2},

(5.47)

s_{\varpi_{k},j}=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}-2j+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}2^{n-2}s^{A_{2n-2}}_{\varpi_{n-1},2j-1},

(5.48)

t_{\varpi_{k},j}=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+2j}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}2^{n-1}t^{A_{2n-2}}_{\varpi_{n-1},2j-1},

(5.49)

\kappa_{\varpi_{k}}(f_{j},e_{j})=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}(2^{n-1}\epsilon^{A_{2n-2}}_{\varpi_{n-1},2j-1}-\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}-2j+1}s_{\varpi_{n-1},j}t_{\varpi_{n-1},j}).

For
k=n-1,n
and
j=n
, we have

(5.50)

s_{\varpi_{n-1},n}=-s_{\varpi_{n},n}=(-1)^{n-1}\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}-n+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}2^{n-2}\deg^{A_{2n-3}}_{\varpi_{n-1}},

(5.51)

\begin{split}t_{\varpi_{n-1},n}=-t_{\varpi_{n},n}=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+n}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}2^{n-2}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\cdot\\
\binom{n^{2}}{n-1+(\sigma(1)-1),\cdots,n-1+(\sigma(n-1)-(n-1)),n-1+\sigma(n)}\end{split},

(5.52)

\kappa_{\varpi_{k}}(f_{j},e_{j})=\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}(2^{-1}\epsilon_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},n}+\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}-n+1}s_{\varpi_{n-1},j}t_{\varpi_{n-1},j})

where

(5.53)

\epsilon_{\varpi_{n-1}+\varpi_{n},n}=2^{n-1}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n-1}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\binom{n(n-1)+1}{n+1+(\sigma(1)-1),n+(\sigma(2)-2),\cdots,n+(\sigma(n-1)-(n-1))}.

Finally, when
n
is even,
k=n-1,n
, we have

(5.54)

\begin{split}\kappa_{\varpi_{n-1}}(f_{n},e_{n/2})=-\kappa_{\varpi_{n}}(f_{n},e_{n/2})=\\
\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}(2^{-1}\kappa_{2\varpi_{n-1}}(f_{n},e_{n/2})-\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1-n}t_{\varpi_{n-1},n}s_{\varpi_{n-1},n/2})\end{split}

(5.55)

\begin{split}\kappa_{\varpi_{n-1}}(f_{n/2},e_{n})=-\kappa_{\varpi_{n}}(f_{n/2},e_{n})=\\
\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}}^{-1}\deg_{\varpi_{n-1}}^{-1}(2^{-1}\kappa_{2\varpi_{n-1}}(f_{n/2},e_{n})-\binom{2d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1}{d_{\varpi_{n-1}}+1-n}t_{\varpi_{n-1},n/2}s_{\varpi_{n-1},n})\end{split}

where

(5.56)

\begin{split}\kappa_{2\varpi_{n-1}}(f_{n},e_{n/2})=2^{n-1}\sum_{a=1}^{n-1}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\cdot\\
\binom{n(n-1)+1}{n-1+(\sigma(1)-1),\cdots,\sigma(a)-a,\cdots,n-1+(\sigma(n-1)-(n-1)),n-1+\sigma(n)}\end{split}

(5.57)

\kappa_{2\varpi_{n-1}}(f_{n/2},e_{n})=-2^{n}t^{A_{2n-3}}_{\varpi_{n-1},n-1}.

When
j\neq n
, these formulas follow immediately from Proposition
5.4
and Proposition
5.1
. The case
j=n
can be treated similarly.

Remark 5.10
.

The formulas in Proposition
5.9
can be compared with the formulas in
[
4
, Theorem 1.3.8]
.

\epsilon_{\varpi_{i},j}
for all semisimple simply-connected groups
\check{G}
of classical types with rank not exceeding 4. These invariants together with the relevant invariants
d_{\lambda},\deg_{\lambda}
are given in Table
1
, Table
2
, Table
3
, Table
4
.

5.1.9.
Exceptional types

In this subsection, we consider the case that
G
is of exceptional type. Using Sagemath
[
8
]
, one can calculate the numbers
(\epsilon_{\lambda,j})_{j=1}^{n}
when
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
is the (quasi-)minuscule or adjoint weight. Table
5
gives the numbers
d_{\lambda},\deg_{\lambda}
and Table
6
gives the numbers
(\epsilon_{\lambda,j})_{j=1}^{n}
.

Remark 5.11
.

Our computation suggests that
\epsilon_{\lambda,i}
are always algebraic integers. Moreover, when
G
is not of type
D_{n}
for
n\geq 4
even, they are always positive integers. We do not have a conceptual explanation for this phenomenon.

\deg_{\lambda},d_{\lambda}
for exceptional types

5.2.
Examples of
b_{\lambda}

In this section, we give examples of the number
b_{\lambda}\in\mathbb{Q}
for
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
.

5.2.1.
Reduction to fundamental weights

The calculation of the numbers
b_{\lambda}
can be reduced to the calculation of a sum of two fundamental weights
\varpi_{i}+\varpi_{j}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
. In fact, the calculation for
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\lambda_{3}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
can be reduced to the calculation of
\lambda_{k},\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
for
1\leq i,j,k\leq 3
. This reduction procedure is provided by Proposition
5.12
.

Proposition 5.12
.

For
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3}\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
, we have

(5.58)

\begin{split}b_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\lambda_{3}}=\\
\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{\lambda_{3}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{1}}}\deg_{\lambda_{1}}b_{\lambda_{2}+\lambda_{3}}+\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{\lambda_{3}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{2}}}\deg_{\lambda_{2}}b_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{3}}\\
+\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{\lambda_{3}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{3}}}\deg_{\lambda_{3}}b_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}-\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{\lambda_{3}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{1}}+1}b_{\lambda_{1}}\deg_{\lambda_{2}+\lambda_{3}}\\
-\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{\lambda_{3}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{2}}+1}b_{\lambda_{2}}\deg_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{3}}-\binom{d_{\lambda_{1}}+d_{\lambda_{2}}+d_{\lambda_{3}}+1}{d_{\lambda_{3}}+1}b_{\lambda_{3}}\deg_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\end{split}.

This follows immediately from Theorem
4.4
.

5.2.2.
Minuscule case

When
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
is minuscule, the number
b_{\lambda}
is particularly easy to determine:

Proposition 5.13
.

When
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
is minuscule, we have

b_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{\min}(\lambda,\lambda)(d_{\lambda}+1)\deg_{\lambda}.

This follows immediately from Theorem
4.4
. The following is a table of the invariants involved in the formula of
b_{\lambda}
for all minuscule weights
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})
:

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c}\check{G}&\textup{Name of $\lambda$}&\kappa_{\min}(\lambda,\lambda)&d_{\lambda}&\deg_{\lambda}\\
\hline\cr A_{n}(n\geq 1)&\varpi_{k}(1\leq k\leq n)&k(n+1-k)/(n+1)&k(n+1-k)&\text{\eqref{eq:Adeg}}\\
B_{n}(n\geq 1)&\textup{spin}&n/2&n(n+1)/2&\text{\eqref{eq:Cspindeg}}\\
C_{n}(n\geq 1)&\textup{standard}&1&2n-1&2\\
D_{n}(n\geq 3)&\textup{standard}&1&2n-2&2\\
D_{n}(n\geq 3)&\textup{half-spin}&n/4&n(n-1)/2&\text{\eqref{eq:Dspindeg}}\\
E_{6}&\textup{minuscule}&4/3&16&78\\
E_{7}&\textup{minuscule}&3/2&27&13110\end{array}.

5.2.3.
Classical types

When
\check{G}
is of classical type, one can write down formulas parallel to those in §
5.1.4
, §
5.1.5
, §
5.1.6
, and §
5.1.7
. We omit the details.

5.2.4.
Exceptional types

When
\check{G}
is of exceptional type, with the help of Sagemath
[
8
]
, we calculate the number
b_{\lambda}
when
\lambda\in X^{*}(\check{T})_{+}
is the (quasi-)minuscule or adjoint weight. The result is collected in Table
7
.

Table 7.
Values of
